{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UQJHgQz8PtmKs8FMKovvbZ","type":"tool_result","content":"2225\t            contract = \"tools/aria-poc/ci_executor_contract_proven.md\"\n2226\t            raise ClaudeCliUnavailable(f\"{exc}; see {contract}\") from exc\n2227\t        raise\n2228\t    # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n2229\t    #\n2230\t    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n2231\t    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n2232\t    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n2233\t    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n2234\t    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n2235\t    # which fails:\n2236\t    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n2237\t    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n2238\t    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n2239\t    #\n2240\t    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n2241\t    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n2242\t    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n2243\t    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n2244\t    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n2245\t    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n2246\t    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n2247\t    #\n2248\t    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n2249\t    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n2250\t    # internal kernel identity fields.\n2251\t    if completed.stdout:\n2252\t        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n2253\t            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n2254\t            request_id=request_id,\n2255\t            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n2256\t            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n2257\t            role=role,\n2258\t            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n2259\t            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n2260\t            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n2261\t        )\n2262\t        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n2263\t        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2264\t        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n2265\t        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2266\t        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n2267\t        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on\n2268\t        # _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY frozen sentinel (V3.1-D2) so a mid-run\n2269\t        # CLAUDE_CLI_MOCK flip cannot rewrite mock-mode classification\n2270\t        # between mint + record sites. request_envelope + tools_dir\n2271\t        # MUST both be supplied for the record to fire — None defaults\n2272\t        # preserve V8 backward-compat for callers that haven't migrated.\n2273\t        if (\n2274\t            completed.returncode == 0\n2275\t            and _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY is False\n2276\t            and request_envelope is not None\n2277\t            and tools_dir is not None\n2278\t        ):\n2279\t            _record_claude_cli_usage(\n2280\t                raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n2281\t                request_envelope=request_envelope,\n2282\t                tools_dir=tools_dir,\n2283\t                role=role,\n2284\t                request_id=request_id,\n2285\t                signer_key_fp=signer_key_fp,\n2286\t            )\n2287\t    _emit_dispatch_summary(\n2288\t        outcome=\"succeeded\" if completed.returncode == 0 else \"failed\",\n2289\t        failure=classify_dispatch_failure(result=completed, phase=\"runtime\"),\n2290\t        exit_code=completed.returncode,\n2291\t    )\n2292\t    if completed.returncode != 0 and completed.stderr:\n2293\t        # The child's own last words, bounded and lease-redacted: a non-zero\n2294\t        # exit with no cause is the operator reading four ledgers to learn\n2295\t        # that the limiter could not reach a bus.\n2296\t        sys.stderr.write(\"claude_stderr_tail: \" + _redact_lease_in_message(completed.stderr[-2000:], None) + \"\\n\")\n2297\t    return completed.returncode\n2298\t\n2299\t\n2300\tdef _record_claude_cli_usage(\n2301\t    *,\n2302\t    raw_stdout: str,\n2303\t    request_envelope: dict[str, Any],\n2304\t    tools_dir: Path,"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6bf14544-6842-4df2-a4f5-61e2b4a46153","uuid":"99cbb396-35f7-4e82-b4c6-ccbf3016a8c7","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:36:29.356Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-autonomy-planner-becc7f34b380/tools/aria-poc/ci_executor.py","content":"            contract = \"tools/aria-poc/ci_executor_contract_proven.md\"\n            raise ClaudeCliUnavailable(f\"{exc}; see {contract}\") from exc\n        raise\n    # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n    #\n    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n    # which fails:\n    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n    #\n    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n    #\n    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n    # internal kernel identity fields.\n    if completed.stdout:\n        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n            request_id=request_id,\n            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n            role=role,\n            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n        )\n        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on\n        # _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY frozen sentinel (V3.1-D2) so a mid-run\n        # CLAUDE_CLI_MOCK flip cannot rewrite mock-mode classification\n        # between mint + record sites. request_envelope + tools_dir\n        # MUST both be supplied for the record to fire — None defaults\n        # preserve V8 backward-compat for callers that haven't migrated.\n        if (\n            completed.returncode == 0\n            and _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY is False\n            and request_envelope is not None\n            and tools_dir is not None\n        ):\n            _record_claude_cli_usage(\n                raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n                request_envelope=request_envelope,\n                tools_dir=tools_dir,\n                role=role,\n                request_id=request_id,\n                signer_key_fp=signer_key_fp,\n            )\n    _emit_dispatch_summary(\n        outcome=\"succeeded\" if completed.returncode == 0 else \"failed\",\n        failure=classify_dispatch_failure(result=completed, phase=\"runtime\"),\n        exit_code=completed.returncode,\n    )\n    if completed.returncode != 0 and completed.stderr:\n        # The child's own last words, bounded and lease-redacted: a non-zero\n        # exit with no cause is the operator reading four ledgers to learn\n        # that the limiter could not reach a bus.\n        sys.stderr.write(\"claude_stderr_tail: \" + _redact_lease_in_message(completed.stderr[-2000:], None) + \"\\n\")\n    return completed.returncode\n\n\ndef _record_claude_cli_usage(\n    *,\n    raw_stdout: str,\n    request_envelope: dict[str, Any],\n    tools_dir: Path,","numLines":80,"startLine":2225,"totalLines":5265}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014x3gFJiPR3sb2JZLegDzGK","type":"tool_result","content":"466\t\n467\tdef _kernel_checkout_root() -> Path:\n468\t    \"\"\"The checkout the RUNNING kernel is part of — where its agent contracts\n469\t    and runtime profiles (`.claude/agents/*.md`) are read from (ARIA-HIGH-146).\n470\t\n471\t    The contract used to be rendered from the workspace (`_REPO_ROOT`): the\n472\t    tree the implementer changes, at the request's `target_sha`. A workspace\n473\t    at an older commit ran the contract of that commit while this executor\n474\t    validated the response against its own; trial eleven's implementer got\n475\t    the 2026-09-12 contract, whose first obligation the sandbox's command\n476\t    policy cannot execute (ARIA-HIGH-147), twice. The contract belongs to\n477\t    the kernel that grades it — the same root ARIA-HIGH-142 serves the\n478\t    in-sandbox clients from.\n479\t    \"\"\"\n480\t    from aria_kernel.claude_settings import kernel_code_root\n481\t\n482\t    return kernel_code_root().parent\n483\t\n484\t\n485\tdef repo_root_provenance_note() -> str | None:\n486\t    \"\"\"The one line a run writes when its repo root was not handed to it,\n487\t    or None when ARIA_WORKSPACE_ROOT named the request's tree.\"\"\"\n488\t    if _REPO_ROOT_SOURCE == \"env\":\n489\t        return None\n490\t    return (\n491\t        f\"{REPO_ROOT_FROM_MODULE_MARKER} ARIA_WORKSPACE_ROOT is unset; the executor's repo root \"\n492\t        f\"is this module's own checkout ({_REPO_ROOT}) — set it when the request's tree is another\"\n493\t    )\n494\t\n495\t\n496\tdef _operator_policy_root(tools_dir: Path) -> Path:\n497\t    \"\"\"The root the operator policy (aria-config/genesis_policy.json) is read from.\n498\t\n499\t    WHY not the workspace: the workspace is the TASK tree — with\n500\t    worktree_per_request (B8) it is a worktree at the request's target_sha,\n501\t    and the policy as of that commit is not the operator's policy. A request\n502\t    minted before an operator decision landed would otherwise be governed by\n503\t    the policy from before the decision (pre-B8: no adaptive block, the\n504\t    metered gate, refused by price), so the decision would reach only\n505\t    requests minted after it. The kernel already answers \"which root's\n506\t    policy\" for the cost gate (ARIA-HIGH-079): the workspace the tools store\n507\t    is BOUND to — the persistent checkout the drain runs from, the fixture\n508\t    repo a test binds its store to. The executor reads the same root, so the\n509\t    admission, the spawn gate and the cost gate read ONE policy.\n510\t    \"\"\"\n511\t    from aria_kernel.tool_registry import bound_workspace_root\n512\t\n513\t    return bound_workspace_root(tools_dir)\n514\t\n515\t\n516\tdef _stage(msg: str) -> None:\n517\t    elapsed = time.monotonic() - _CI_T0\n518\t    sys.stderr.write(f\"[ci-stage t={elapsed:7.2f}s] {msg}\\n\")\n519\t    sys.stderr.flush()\n520\t\n521\t\n522\tdef _write_dispatch_summary(\n523\t    *, route: DispatchRoute, request_id: str, outcome: str,\n524\t    failure: DispatchFailure | None, exit_code: int | None,\n525\t) -> None:\n526\t    \"\"\"One sanitized ``aria/dispatch-result/v1`` summary for a terminal path.\n527\t\n528\t    The summary is telemetry: a dispatch outcome must never fail because the\n529\t    telemetry channel did, so an unwritable summary is named on stderr and\n530\t    the outcome stands. Every terminal path — inside ``invoke_claude_cli``\n531\t    and every by-design exit of ``_main`` before it — writes through here.\n532\t    \"\"\"\n533\t    try:\n534\t        emit_dispatch_result_summary(\n535\t            route=route, request_id=request_id, outcome=outcome,\n536\t            failure=failure, exit_code=exit_code,\n537\t        )\n538\t    except (OSError, ValueError) as summary_exc:\n539\t        sys.stderr.write(f\"dispatch_summary_unwritable: {summary_exc}\\n\")\n540\t\n541\t\n542\t# A refusal is a legitimate terminal, not a build failure: the request keeps\n543\t# its budget, the run stays green, and the SUMMARY — never the exit code —\n544\t# says it was not a success.\n545\tREFUSAL_EXIT_CODE = 0\n546\t\n547\t\n548\tdef _dispatch_route_for(request: dict[str, Any], *, target_agent: str) -> DispatchRoute:\n549\t    \"\"\"ARIA-HIGH-002 — the route this child dispatches (or would dispatch) on.\n550\t\n551\t    The trusted request envelope names the agent/role; the frontmatter SSoT\n552\t    in the CODE root resolves the model and the fleet resolves the provider.\n553\t    The code root, not the workspace: the drain resolves the same route from\n554\t    the same checkout before it claims, and the two must key one circuit.\n555\t    ``target_agent`` is the dispatched argv agent, the fallback when the\n556\t    envelope (a mocked one) names none.\n557\t    \"\"\"\n558\t    return resolve_dispatch_route(\n559\t        request={\"role\": request.get(\"role\"),\n560\t                 \"target_agent\": str(request.get(\"target_agent\") or target_agent)},\n561\t        repo_root=_CODE_ROOT,\n562\t    )\n563\t\n564\t\n565\tdef _refuse_dispatch(\n566\t    *, request: dict[str, Any], request_id: str, target_agent: str, reason: str,\n567\t    failure_class: str = \"policy_violation\", retryable: bool = False,\n568\t) -> int:\n569\t    \"\"\"A by-design non-dispatch, NAMED in the child's summary (B8, 2026-09-12).\n570\t\n571\t    WHY: the child exited 0 with no summary on every refusal decided before\n572\t    ``invoke_claude_cli`` — the native admission's task-binding refusal, the\n573\t    fleet's ``no_eligible_provider``, the budget signal, the operator cancel,\n574\t    the recovery escalation — and the drain read \"exit 0, no summary\" as a\n575\t    drained success. Under ``managed_subscription`` the task binding refuses\n576\t    whenever main has moved past a request's ``target_sha``, so a night of\n577\t    orphaned requests read as a green drain (verifier B8 MUST FIX 1). The\n578\t    drain now names a summary-less child as ``child_without_summary`` and\n579\t    counts nothing but a ``succeeded`` summary as drained; this is the other\n580\t    half: every by-design exit says what it was.\n581\t\n582\t    WHAT: outcome ``refused``, ``failure_class``/``retryable`` as the\n583\t    refusal's own kind — ``policy_violation`` / not retryable by default\n584\t    (the dispatch met one of the executor's own admission policies), and\n585\t    what the fleet's refusal table declares for a halted admission\n586\t    (``harness_unavailable`` / retryable: the host, not the request, and\n587\t    the daemon retries it after a back-off) — and ``reason`` as the detail\n588\t    code, the closed vocabulary the drain and the operator read. Returns\n589\t    the exit code a refusal carries.\n590\t    \"\"\"\n591\t    _write_dispatch_summary(\n592\t        route=_dispatch_route_for(request, target_agent=target_agent),\n593\t        request_id=request_id, outcome=\"refused\",\n594\t        failure=DispatchFailure(\n595\t            failure_class=failure_class, retryable=retryable, detail_code=reason,"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6bf14544-6842-4df2-a4f5-61e2b4a46153","uuid":"a9d60f41-5289-4fe6-8601-5e2fed18d72f","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:37:45.239Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-autonomy-planner-becc7f34b380/tools/aria-poc/ci_executor.py","content":"\ndef _kernel_checkout_root() -> Path:\n    \"\"\"The checkout the RUNNING kernel is part of — where its agent contracts\n    and runtime profiles (`.claude/agents/*.md`) are read from (ARIA-HIGH-146).\n\n    The contract used to be rendered from the workspace (`_REPO_ROOT`): the\n    tree the implementer changes, at the request's `target_sha`. A workspace\n    at an older commit ran the contract of that commit while this executor\n    validated the response against its own; trial eleven's implementer got\n    the 2026-09-12 contract, whose first obligation the sandbox's command\n    policy cannot execute (ARIA-HIGH-147), twice. The contract belongs to\n    the kernel that grades it — the same root ARIA-HIGH-142 serves the\n    in-sandbox clients from.\n    \"\"\"\n    from aria_kernel.claude_settings import kernel_code_root\n\n    return kernel_code_root().parent\n\n\ndef repo_root_provenance_note() -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"The one line a run writes when its repo root was not handed to it,\n    or None when ARIA_WORKSPACE_ROOT named the request's tree.\"\"\"\n    if _REPO_ROOT_SOURCE == \"env\":\n        return None\n    return (\n        f\"{REPO_ROOT_FROM_MODULE_MARKER} ARIA_WORKSPACE_ROOT is unset; the executor's repo root \"\n        f\"is this module's own checkout ({_REPO_ROOT}) — set it when the request's tree is another\"\n    )\n\n\ndef _operator_policy_root(tools_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"The root the operator policy (aria-config/genesis_policy.json) is read from.\n\n    WHY not the workspace: the workspace is the TASK tree — with\n    worktree_per_request (B8) it is a worktree at the request's target_sha,\n    and the policy as of that commit is not the operator's policy. A request\n    minted before an operator decision landed would otherwise be governed by\n    the policy from before the decision (pre-B8: no adaptive block, the\n    metered gate, refused by price), so the decision would reach only\n    requests minted after it. The kernel already answers \"which root's\n    policy\" for the cost gate (ARIA-HIGH-079): the workspace the tools store\n    is BOUND to — the persistent checkout the drain runs from, the fixture\n    repo a test binds its store to. The executor reads the same root, so the\n    admission, the spawn gate and the cost gate read ONE policy.\n    \"\"\"\n    from aria_kernel.tool_registry import bound_workspace_root\n\n    return bound_workspace_root(tools_dir)\n\n\ndef _stage(msg: str) -> None:\n    elapsed = time.monotonic() - _CI_T0\n    sys.stderr.write(f\"[ci-stage t={elapsed:7.2f}s] {msg}\\n\")\n    sys.stderr.flush()\n\n\ndef _write_dispatch_summary(\n    *, route: DispatchRoute, request_id: str, outcome: str,\n    failure: DispatchFailure | None, exit_code: int | None,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"One sanitized ``aria/dispatch-result/v1`` summary for a terminal path.\n\n    The summary is telemetry: a dispatch outcome must never fail because the\n    telemetry channel did, so an unwritable summary is named on stderr and\n    the outcome stands. Every terminal path — inside ``invoke_claude_cli``\n    and every by-design exit of ``_main`` before it — writes through here.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        emit_dispatch_result_summary(\n            route=route, request_id=request_id, outcome=outcome,\n            failure=failure, exit_code=exit_code,\n        )\n    except (OSError, ValueError) as summary_exc:\n        sys.stderr.write(f\"dispatch_summary_unwritable: {summary_exc}\\n\")\n\n\n# A refusal is a legitimate terminal, not a build failure: the request keeps\n# its budget, the run stays green, and the SUMMARY — never the exit code —\n# says it was not a success.\nREFUSAL_EXIT_CODE = 0\n\n\ndef _dispatch_route_for(request: dict[str, Any], *, target_agent: str) -> DispatchRoute:\n    \"\"\"ARIA-HIGH-002 — the route this child dispatches (or would dispatch) on.\n\n    The trusted request envelope names the agent/role; the frontmatter SSoT\n    in the CODE root resolves the model and the fleet resolves the provider.\n    The code root, not the workspace: the drain resolves the same route from\n    the same checkout before it claims, and the two must key one circuit.\n    ``target_agent`` is the dispatched argv agent, the fallback when the\n    envelope (a mocked one) names none.\n    \"\"\"\n    return resolve_dispatch_route(\n        request={\"role\": request.get(\"role\"),\n                 \"target_agent\": str(request.get(\"target_agent\") or target_agent)},\n        repo_root=_CODE_ROOT,\n    )\n\n\ndef _refuse_dispatch(\n    *, request: dict[str, Any], request_id: str, target_agent: str, reason: str,\n    failure_class: str = \"policy_violation\", retryable: bool = False,\n) -> int:\n    \"\"\"A by-design non-dispatch, NAMED in the child's summary (B8, 2026-09-12).\n\n    WHY: the child exited 0 with no summary on every refusal decided before\n    ``invoke_claude_cli`` — the native admission's task-binding refusal, the\n    fleet's ``no_eligible_provider``, the budget signal, the operator cancel,\n    the recovery escalation — and the drain read \"exit 0, no summary\" as a\n    drained success. Under ``managed_subscription`` the task binding refuses\n    whenever main has moved past a request's ``target_sha``, so a night of\n    orphaned requests read as a green drain (verifier B8 MUST FIX 1). The\n    drain now names a summary-less child as ``child_without_summary`` and\n    counts nothing but a ``succeeded`` summary as drained; this is the other\n    half: every by-design exit says what it was.\n\n    WHAT: outcome ``refused``, ``failure_class``/``retryable`` as the\n    refusal's own kind — ``policy_violation`` / not retryable by default\n    (the dispatch met one of the executor's own admission policies), and\n    what the fleet's refusal table declares for a halted admission\n    (``harness_unavailable`` / retryable: the host, not the request, and\n    the daemon retries it after a back-off) — and ``reason`` as the detail\n    code, the closed vocabulary the drain and the operator read. Returns\n    the exit code a refusal carries.\n    \"\"\"\n    _write_dispatch_summary(\n        route=_dispatch_route_for(request, target_agent=target_agent),\n        request_id=request_id, outcome=\"refused\",\n        failure=DispatchFailure(\n            failure_class=failure_class, retryable=retryable, detail_code=reason,","numLines":130,"startLine":466,"totalLines":5265}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01H42LxFdVNKdygL6igjaiqP","type":"tool_result","content":"4150\t    # evidence_refs) PLUS the §B.5 ledger-hash anchors\n4151\t    # (claim_ledger_hash / request_ledger_hash) inside the same\n4152\t    # exclusive-lock window that performed the claim CAS. The pre-§B.3\n4153\t    # second-fetch via ``agent-invocations list --request-id`` opened a\n4154\t    # race window: between claim-success and the list-fetch, a release\n4155\t    # or reaper sweep could mutate the request row and the executor\n4156\t    # would operate on a stale envelope. Reading from the fused\n4157\t    # response closes the race AND eliminates one subprocess hop per\n4158\t    # cycle (lower latency).\n4159\t    # The envelope is the kernel's OWN projection of the claim response, not a\n4160\t    # copy maintained here. This function used to hand-build the dict —\n4161\t    # `claim.get(\"forbidden_scope\") or []`, no `repository_map` — which is the\n4162\t    # same defect the kernel-side fusion fix closed, one layer up: `or []`\n4163\t    # turns absence into an empty value, the renderer distinguishes the two,\n4164\t    # and the dropped map deleted the whole `## Repository map` section from\n4165\t    # the re-render. Net effect, measured on run 31330288849: the binding\n4166\t    # check compared the hash of the row against the hash of this copy and\n4167\t    # failed for every request that carried a map — after the kernel fix had\n4168\t    # already landed. One projection, owned by the kernel, ends the class.\n4169\t    if _fuse_prompt_envelope is None:\n4170\t        sys.stderr.write(\"kernel_prompt_renderer_unavailable\\n\")\n4171\t        _release_claim(\n4172\t            tools_dir=tools_dir, repo=repo, claim_id=claim_id,\n4173\t            agent_id=agent_id, lease_token=lease_token,\n4174\t            reason=\"kernel_prompt_renderer_unavailable\",\n4175\t        )\n4176\t        return 1\n4177\t    request_envelope = _fuse_prompt_envelope(claim)\n4178\t    if native_runtime is not None:\n4179\t        if native_runtime.request[\"ledger_hash\"] != claim[\"request_ledger_hash\"]:\n4180\t            sys.stderr.write(\"native_runtime_request_hash_changed\\n\")\n4181\t            _release_claim(tools_dir=tools_dir, repo=repo, claim_id=claim_id,\n4182\t                           agent_id=agent_id, lease_token=lease_token,\n4183\t                           reason=\"native_runtime_task_binding_unavailable\")\n4184\t            return 1\n4185\t        request_envelope[\"target_sha\"] = native_runtime.request[\"target_sha\"]\n4186\t    request_envelope.setdefault(\"request_id\", request_id)\n4187\t    # Operational anchors, NOT prompt material: the renderer never reads\n4188\t    # these, so carrying them cannot perturb the binding. claim/request\n4189\t    # ledger hashes feed the §B.5 metadata-tamper check.\n4190\t    for anchor in (\"claim_ledger_hash\", \"request_ledger_hash\"):\n4191\t        if claim.get(anchor) is not None:\n4192\t            request_envelope[anchor] = claim[anchor]\n4193\t    if not request_envelope.get(\"expected_output_path\"):\n4194\t        sys.stderr.write(\n4195\t            f\"request_envelope_missing_expected_output_path: \"\n4196\t            f\"request_id={request_id}\\n\"\n4197\t        )\n4198\t        _release_claim(\n4199\t            tools_dir=tools_dir, repo=repo, claim_id=claim_id,\n4200\t            agent_id=agent_id, lease_token=lease_token,\n4201\t            reason=\"request_envelope_missing_expected_output_path\",\n4202\t        )\n4203\t        return 1\n4204\t    if not request_envelope.get(\"role\"):\n4205\t        sys.stderr.write(\n4206\t            f\"request_envelope_missing_role: request_id={request_id}\\n\"\n4207\t        )\n4208\t        _release_claim(\n4209\t            tools_dir=tools_dir, repo=repo, claim_id=claim_id,"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6bf14544-6842-4df2-a4f5-61e2b4a46153","uuid":"5411113f-fa09-4006-8177-2970cbd83ed4","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:37:53.336Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-autonomy-planner-becc7f34b380/tools/aria-poc/ci_executor.py","content":"    # evidence_refs) PLUS the §B.5 ledger-hash anchors\n    # (claim_ledger_hash / request_ledger_hash) inside the same\n    # exclusive-lock window that performed the claim CAS. The pre-§B.3\n    # second-fetch via ``agent-invocations list --request-id`` opened a\n    # race window: between claim-success and the list-fetch, a release\n    # or reaper sweep could mutate the request row and the executor\n    # would operate on a stale envelope. Reading from the fused\n    # response closes the race AND eliminates one subprocess hop per\n    # cycle (lower latency).\n    # The envelope is the kernel's OWN projection of the claim response, not a\n    # copy maintained here. This function used to hand-build the dict —\n    # `claim.get(\"forbidden_scope\") or []`, no `repository_map` — which is the\n    # same defect the kernel-side fusion fix closed, one layer up: `or []`\n    # turns absence into an empty value, the renderer distinguishes the two,\n    # and the dropped map deleted the whole `## Repository map` section from\n    # the re-render. Net effect, measured on run 31330288849: the binding\n    # check compared the hash of the row against the hash of this copy and\n    # failed for every request that carried a map — after the kernel fix had\n    # already landed. One projection, owned by the kernel, ends the class.\n    if _fuse_prompt_envelope is None:\n        sys.stderr.write(\"kernel_prompt_renderer_unavailable\\n\")\n        _release_claim(\n            tools_dir=tools_dir, repo=repo, claim_id=claim_id,\n            agent_id=agent_id, lease_token=lease_token,\n            reason=\"kernel_prompt_renderer_unavailable\",\n        )\n        return 1\n    request_envelope = _fuse_prompt_envelope(claim)\n    if native_runtime is not None:\n        if native_runtime.request[\"ledger_hash\"] != claim[\"request_ledger_hash\"]:\n            sys.stderr.write(\"native_runtime_request_hash_changed\\n\")\n            _release_claim(tools_dir=tools_dir, repo=repo, claim_id=claim_id,\n                           agent_id=agent_id, lease_token=lease_token,\n                           reason=\"native_runtime_task_binding_unavailable\")\n            return 1\n        request_envelope[\"target_sha\"] = native_runtime.request[\"target_sha\"]\n    request_envelope.setdefault(\"request_id\", request_id)\n    # Operational anchors, NOT prompt material: the renderer never reads\n    # these, so carrying them cannot perturb the binding. claim/request\n    # ledger hashes feed the §B.5 metadata-tamper check.\n    for anchor in (\"claim_ledger_hash\", \"request_ledger_hash\"):\n        if claim.get(anchor) is not None:\n            request_envelope[anchor] = claim[anchor]\n    if not request_envelope.get(\"expected_output_path\"):\n        sys.stderr.write(\n            f\"request_envelope_missing_expected_output_path: \"\n            f\"request_id={request_id}\\n\"\n        )\n        _release_claim(\n            tools_dir=tools_dir, repo=repo, claim_id=claim_id,\n            agent_id=agent_id, lease_token=lease_token,\n            reason=\"request_envelope_missing_expected_output_path\",\n        )\n        return 1\n    if not request_envelope.get(\"role\"):\n        sys.stderr.write(\n            f\"request_envelope_missing_role: request_id={request_id}\\n\"\n        )\n        _release_claim(\n            tools_dir=tools_dir, repo=repo, claim_id=claim_id,","numLines":60,"startLine":4150,"totalLines":5265}}}
